### Fig. 110: View Of Caliper To Caliper Mounting Bracket Courtesy ofGENERAL MOTORS CORP.
`chevrolet_trailblazer_en_fa21947f` | trailblazer | pdf_manual | tier:4 | lang:en | layer:brakes | p:1 | doc_type:service_manual

9. Install the rear brake caliper assembly. Refer to Brake Caliper Replacement - Rear 10. Install the tire and wheel assembly. Refer to Tire and Wheel Removal and Installation in Suspension. 11. Lower the vehicle. Refer to Lifting and Jacking the Vehicle in General Information.

CAUTION: Refer to Firm Brake Pedal Caution in Cautions and Notices.

12. Pump the brake pedal slowly and firmly in order to seat the brake pads.

### BRAKE ROTOR REPLACEMENT FRONT
`chevrolet_trailblazer_en_2084030a` | trailblazer | pdf_manual | tier:4 | lang:en | layer:general | p:1 | doc_type:service_manual

Removal Procedure

Tools Required

J 42450-A Wheel Hub Cleaning Kit. See Special Tools and Equipment.

CAUTION: Refer to Brake Dust Caution in Cautions and Notices.

![](images/d9ae96e2d63b2e587fb1d3fcf14577cb6f99fa8b8c9e02aa1508ff2f4f113e0b.jpg)

### Fig. 111: View Of Half-Full Master Cylinder Reservoir Courtesy ofGENERAL MOTORS CORP
`chevrolet_trailblazer_en_8e5cc076` | trailblazer | pdf_manual | tier:4 | lang:en | layer:general | p:1 | doc_type:service_manual

1. Inspect the brake fluid level in the brake master cylinder reservoir. 2. If the brake fluid is midway between the maximum-full point and the minimum allowable level, no brake fluid needs to be removed from the reservoir before proceeding.

![](images/fa37d43592870476abeb08bd099f493ed1a655fdf52083edd74449a357591a0d.jpg)

### Fig. 112: View Of Full Master Cylinder Reservoir MAX And MIN Marks Courtesy ofGENERAL MOTORS CORP.
`chevrolet_trailblazer_en_457991a9` | trailblazer | pdf_manual | tier:4 | lang:en | layer:brakes | p:1 | doc_type:service_manual

3. If the brake fluid level is higher than midway between thew maximum-full point and the minimum allowable level, using a appropriate tool, remove the brake fluid to the midway point before proceeding.
4. Raise the vehicle. Refer to Lifting and Jacking the Vehicle in General Information.
5. Remove the tire and wheel assembly. Refer to Tire and Wheel Removal and Installation in Tires and Wheels.

![](images/b100e19a08b2d156368fdae7f5511e366e9da9a107998cfae599bc248958dcc7.jpg)
Fig, 113: Compressing Front Brake Caliper Piston Courtesy of GENERAL MOTORS CORP.

6. Compress the front brake caliper piston.

1. Install a large C-clamp over the top of the brake caliper housing and against the back of the outboard brake pad.
2. Slowly tighten the C-clamp until the piston pushes into the brake caliper enough to slide the brake caliper off the rotor.
3. Remove the C-clamp from the brake caliper.

![](images/cb7b7351f8fd54436fec14011239590db40f95b9050a218780dccb48e746ce7d.jpg)
Fig. 114: Caliper Mounting View Courtesy ofGENERAL MOTORS CORP.

7. Remove the mounting bolts from the brake caliper mounting bracket.

![](images/8be65b9de259ce845ddc9233dadcd0ade7ac14bf6fb4913e61c1fdd8fed54ac1.jpg)

### Fig. 115: Removing/Installing Brake Caliper & Mounting Bracket Courtesy ofGENERAL MOTORS CORP.
`chevrolet_trailblazer_en_23248dd3` | trailblazer | pdf_manual | tier:4 | lang:en | layer:brakes | p:1 | doc_type:service_manual

IMPORTANT: When performing the following service procedure, the front brake caliperassembly and the front brake caliper mounting bracket can be removed as an assembly.

8. Remove the brake caliper and mounting bracket assembly. Refer to Brake Caliper Bracket Replacement Front.

![](images/bab9fcb020e0e77fb772f474d5f867e90951e55f1e44e65e5a0d3fd079955467.jpg)

### Fig. 116: Removing Retaining Clips From Brake Rotor Courtesy of GENERAL MOTORS CORP.
`chevrolet_trailblazer_en_7fbe5605` | trailblazer | pdf_manual | tier:4 | lang:en | layer:general | p:1 | doc_type:service_manual

9. Remove the retaining clips from the brake rotor.

![](images/11a21c685920528a6e7f25049d5bf3f624aefa5a5562a67f7e99d6d17c2c6ebc.jpg)
Fig. 117: Removing/Installing Front Brake Rotor Courtesy of GENERAL MOTORS CORP.

### NOTE:
`chevrolet_trailblazer_en_0296a949` | trailblazer | pdf_manual | tier:4 | lang:en | layer:general | p:1 | doc_type:service_manual

Whenever the brake rotor has been separated from the wheel bearing flange, clean any rust or foreign material from the mating surface of the rotor and flange with the J 42450 hub cleaning kit. Failure to do this may result in increased lateral runout of the rotor and brake pulsation.

IMPORTANT: If the brake rotor is not going to be replaced but just removed from the front hub, mark the relationship of the brake rotor and a wheel stud.

10. Remove the front brake rotor from the wheel hub.

![](images/6d600c4bd6525dd89379e56838e9624927d5aed1c64399076f4ddb8b3c96bc4c.jpg)
Fig. 118: Cleaning Brake Rotor Contact Area Courtesy of GENERAL MOTORS CORP.

11. Using the J 42450-A clean the brake rotor contact area on the front hub. See Special Tools and Equipment.

Installation Procedure

![](images/aa7316d53d91ff3e44079022c8f0119a5816c8f487e548f8a9ccc529cf6abe44.jpg)
Fig. 119: Removing/Installing Front Brake Rotor Courtesy ofGENERAL MOTORS CORP.

IMPORTANT: Ensure that the relationship marks on the brake rotor and the wheel stud are aligned before installing the brake rotor.

1. Install the brake rotor to the wheel hub.

![](images/c84599066b50511280e86fec545c64d554d6475c438e2be7cff7913c28efed4a.jpg)

### Fig. 120: Removing/Installing Brake Caliper & Mounting Bracket Courtesy of GENERAL MOTORS CORP.
`chevrolet_trailblazer_en_8c475606` | trailblazer | pdf_manual | tier:4 | lang:en | layer:brakes | p:1 | doc_type:service_manual

2. Install the brake caliper assembly to the steering knuckle.

![](images/9c3848ede94bd1554854659325b7aa1afa17fe39a0a73561ab864c2c247c8e93.jpg)
Fig. 121: Caliper Mounting View Courtesy of GENERAL MOTORS CORP.

NOTE: Refer to Fastener Notice in Cautions and Notices.

3. Install the brake caliper mounting bolt.

Tighten: Tighten the brake caliper bracket mounting bolt to 180 N.m (133 lb ft).

4. Install the tire and wheel assembly. Refer to Tire and Wheel Removal and Installation in Tires and Wheels.
5. Lower the vehicle. Refer to Lifting and Jacking the Vehicle in General Information.
6. Fill the brake mastercylinder to the proper level. Refer to Master Cylinder Reservoir Filling in Hydraulic Brakes.

### CAUTION: Refer to Firm Brake Pedal Caution in Cautions and Notices.
`chevrolet_trailblazer_en_6b229afd` | trailblazer | pdf_manual | tier:4 | lang:en | layer:interior | p:1 | doc_type:service_manual

7. Pump the brake pedal slowly and firmly in order to seat the brake pads.
8. Burnish the replaced or refinished rotors. Refer to Burnishing Pads and Rotors.

### Tools Required
`chevrolet_trailblazer_en_40a196a5` | trailblazer | pdf_manual | tier:4 | lang:en | layer:general | p:1 | doc_type:service_manual

J 2619-01 Slide Hammer. See Special Tools and Equipment.
J 42450-A Wheel Hub Resurfacing Kit. See Special Tools and Equipment.
J21177-A Drum to Brake Shoe Clearance Gage. See Special Tools and Equipment.
J-46277 Rotor Removal Tool. See Special Tools and Equipment.

### Fig. 122: View OfHalf-Full Master Cylinder Reservoir Courtesy ofGENERAL MOTORS CORP.
`chevrolet_trailblazer_en_8e5cc076` | trailblazer | pdf_manual | tier:4 | lang:en | layer:general | p:1 | doc_type:service_manual

1. Inspect the brake fluid level in the brake master cylinder reservoir. 2. If the brake fluid is midway between the maximum-full point and the minimum allowable level, no brake fluid needs to be removed from the reservoir before proceeding.

![](images/3a145537f9c89cc519e46597b23ff446a4538b42ac1a154f7b8a3daf055c04e7.jpg)

### Fig. 123: View Of Full Master Cylinder Reservoir MAX And MIN Marks Courtesy ofGENERAL MOTORS CORP.
`chevrolet_trailblazer_en_ba552721` | trailblazer | pdf_manual | tier:4 | lang:en | layer:brakes | p:1 | doc_type:service_manual

3. If the brake fluid level is higher than midway between the maximum-full point and the minimum allowable level, using an appropriate tool, remove the brake fluid to the midway point before proceeding.
4. Raise the vehicle. Refer to Lifting and Jacking the Vehicle in General Information.
5. Remove the tire and wheel assembly. Refer to Tire and Wheel Removal and Installation in Tires and Wheels.

![](images/f966dbb9e5fbd983a2e988b6ca056e8be6dc44a66cb22b1e2750784697feb1ac.jpg)
Fig. 124: Compressing Rear Brake Caliper Piston Courtesy of GENERAL MOTORS CORP.

6. Compress the rear brake caliper pistons.

1. Install a large C-clamp over the top of the brake caliper housing and against the back of the outboard brake pad.
2. Slowly tighten the C-clamp until the pistons push into the brake caliper enough to slide the brake caliper off the rotor.
3. Remove the C-clamp from the brake caliper.

![](images/8296780751c13c83138d502f985dce1b2ffecaf7d262f96a3876710c625169ab.jpg)
Fig. 125: View Of Caliper Mounting Bracket & Rotor Courtesy ofGENERAL MOTORS CORP.

7. Remove the rear brake caliper mounting bracket.

![](images/a25793a02d7f120f6a013b6813c277d395a21c0c2e3a37bfae7125c3b91dbb63.jpg)

### Fig. 126: Removing Brake Rotor Retaining Clips Courtesy of GENERAL MOTORS CORP.
`chevrolet_trailblazer_en_e37f4f86` | trailblazer | pdf_manual | tier:4 | lang:en | layer:general | p:1 | doc_type:service_manual

8. Remove the brake rotor retaining clips, if equipped.

![](images/cc1b09011d6bff12fe5981a2d3366d0c14ce90daf03e66c2925361bb782aaaa6.jpg)

### Fig. 127: View Of Rotor From Hub Assembly Removal Using J-46277 &J2619-01 Courtesy of GENERAL MOTORS
`chevrolet_trailblazer_en_d110d885` | trailblazer | pdf_manual | tier:4 | lang:en | layer:general | p:1 | doc_type:service_manual

9. If the brake rotor is stuck, perform the following:

1. Assemble J-46277 to J 2619-01 See Special Tools and Equipment.
2. Insert J-46277 between the rotor friction surfaces in the vent section of the rotor. See Special Tools and Equipment. DO NOT place the J-46277 on the rotor friction surface.
3. Using the J-46277 and J2619-01 release the rotor from the hub assembly See Special Tools and Equipment.

![](images/e831977caeea70393367cd4c08f6428c1e1e5addf7f319983b56a893c2e635a9.jpg)

Fig. 128: Separating Rotor From Axle Flange Courtesy of GENERAL MOTORS CORP.

NOTE: Whenever the rotor has been separated from the axle flange, clean any rust or foreign material from the mating surface of the axle flange and brake rotor. Failure to do this may result in increased lateral runout of the rotor and brake pulsation.

IMPORTANT: If the brake rotor is not going to be replaced but just removed from the front hub to be refinished, mark the brake rotor left or right and the relationship of the brake rotor and a wheel stud. In doing so, it will reduce the possibility of vibration in the brake system.

10. Remove the brake rotor.

![](images/8c504703d80a53d59afeaf1a5264e418edc7b29db55439879883843e768f92fb.jpg)
Fig. 129: Cleaning Brake Rotor To Rear Axle Flange Contact Area Courtesy ofGENERAL MOTORS CORP.

11. Using the J 42450-A clean the brake rotor to the rear axle flange contact area. See Special Tools and Equipment.

12. Refinish the rotor, if necessary Refer to Brake Rotor Refinishing

13. Inspect the park brake components. If any of the following conditions are found, replace the affected parts.

Bent or broken hold down spring Broken, cracked, or worn brake shoe lining Bent or damaged brake shoe Worn, bent, or damaged backing plate

### Installation Proccdure
`chevrolet_trailblazer_en_d2462083` | trailblazer | pdf_manual | tier:4 | lang:en | layer:general | p:1 | doc_type:service_manual

1. Clean the park brake shoes using denatured alcohol.
2. Dry the park shoes using non-lubricated, filtered air.
3. Lubricate the contact area between the park brake shoe and the backing plate with high temperature silicone brake lubricant.

### IMPORTANT: It maybe necessary to readjust the park brake shoes to allow the brake rotor to be reinst
`chevrolet_trailblazer_en_474d764b` | trailblazer | pdf_manual | tier:4 | lang:en | layer:brakes | p:1 | doc_type:service_manual

4. Adjust the park brake. Refer to Park Brake Adjustment in Park Brake.

![](images/d1417797363d4c7693e79014c039c2568c9fbb1c6c910a7fdfcc1e39df610b58.jpg)

Fig. 130: View Of Rotor To Hub Courtesy ofGENERAL MOTORS CORP.

IMPORTANT: Ensure that the rotor to axle flange relationships marks are aligned, if the brake rotor has been refinished, after the brake rotor is installed.

5. Install the brake rotor by slowly turning the rotor while pushing the rotor on the axle flange.

![](images/9bc73e5d4d63398e50746badaa66af85d866be8c4d3ceaeae871e08b9b36e6d7.jpg)
Fig. 131: View Of Caliper Mounting Bracket & Rotor Courtesy ofGENERAL MOTORS CORP.

6. Install the rear brake caliper mounting bracket. Refer to Brake Caliper Bracket Replacement Rear.

7. Install the tire and wheel assembly Refer to Tire and Wheel Removal and Installation in Tires and Wheels.

8. Lower the vehicle. Refer to Lifting and Jacking the Vehicle in General Information.

9. Refill the brake master cylinder reservoir, if needed. Refer to Master Cylinder Reservoir Filling in Hydraulic Brakes.

CAUTION: Do not move the vehicle until a firm brake pedal is obtained. Failure to obtain a firm pedal before moving vehicle may result in personal injury.

10. Pump the brake pedal slowly and firmly in order to seat the brake pads.
11. Burnish replaced or refinished rotors. Refer to Burnishing Pads and Rotors.

### CAUTION: Refer to Brake Dust Caution in Cautions and Notices.
`chevrolet_trailblazer_en_0bba2a60` | trailblazer | pdf_manual | tier:4 | lang:en | layer:general | p:1 | doc_type:service_manual

Removal Procedurc

![](images/41ae244b4e35808c0d0d6da8903a8c5ae581ae5a3ae9e59a58d8318ed0923378.jpg)
Fig. 132: Splash Shield (15 Series) Courtesy of GENERAL MOTORS CORP.

1. Raise the vehicle. Refer toLifting and Jacking the Vehicle in General Information.
2. Remove the tire and wheel assembly. Refer toTire and Wheel Removal and Installation in Suspension.
3. Remove the front hub and bearing assembly. Refer toWheel Hub, Bearing, and Seal Replacement (RWD) or Wheel Hub, Bearing, and Seal Replacement (4WD) in Suspension.
4. Remove the retaining bolts for the splash shield.
5. Remove the splash shield.

### Fig. 133: Splash Shield (15 Series) Courtesy of GENERAL MOTORS CORP.
`chevrolet_trailblazer_en_6668842c` | trailblazer | pdf_manual | tier:4 | lang:en | layer:general | p:1 | doc_type:service_manual

1. Install the splash shield.

2. Install the splash shield retaining bolts, same as wheel hub and bearing.

3. Install the front hub and bearing assembly. Refer toWheel Hub, Bearing, and Seal Replacement (RWD) or Wheel Hub, Bearing, and Seal Replacement (4WD) in Suspension.

4. Install the tire and wheel assembly. Refer toTire and Wheel Removal and Installation in Tires and Wheels.

5. Lower the vehicle. Refer toLifting and Jacking the Vehicle in General Information

### Removal Procedure
`chevrolet_trailblazer_en_88697146` | trailblazer | pdf_manual | tier:4 | lang:en | layer:brakes | p:1 | doc_type:service_manual

![](images/5cdca2bfffbe7a3610b8a0c42dce3d4887ffae6bb1130d8e56101c23e7b96d5f.jpg)
Fig. 134: View Of Parking Brake Cable Courtesy ofGENERAL MOTORS CORP.

1. Raise the vehicle. Refer to Lifting and Jacking the Vehicle in General Information.
2. Remove the tire and wheel assembly. Refer to Tire and Wheel Removal and Installation in Tires and Wheels.
3. Remove the park brake cable from the rear brake caliper. Refer to Park Brake Cable Replacement in Park Brake.

![](images/bea4aad8ead6108506da46bfd134309f7bc74499d0fd623b7e8c3ef3f911008a.jpg)
Fig. 135: View Of Caliper Bracket Courtesy of GENERAL MOTORS CORP.

4. Remove the brake caliper and bracket assembly. Refer to Brake Caliper Bracket Replacement Rear.

![](images/73ec08e962aad2de1a2d65342cbac0e31bb859fe6c56c9f6b8c7db7811399d0f.jpg)

### Fig. 136: View Of Rotor To Hub Courtesy of GENERAL MOTORS CORP.
`chevrolet_trailblazer_en_4be75013` | trailblazer | pdf_manual | tier:4 | lang:en | layer:general | p:1 | doc_type:service_manual

5. Remove the rear brake rotor.

![](images/0d80d17f72f777fdeaeb591f9c3d9f9ccffc2890e93aad98ce0e3765ea926276.jpg)
Fig. 137: View Of Park Brake Shoe Courtesy of GENERAL MOTORS CORP.

6. Remove the park brake shoe.

![](images/300ffd17302468ffa364d7a929800b67078a3a8d372f6b7ec3f779e5f02021ac.jpg)
Fig. 138: Removing Rear Drive Axle Courtesy ofGENERAL MOTORS CORP.

7. Remove the rear drive axle. Refer to Rear Axle Shaft Replacement in Rear Drive Axle.

![](images/4496e56cb8a33d6dfea69c1272ea5b1a18af51ed5c9a0506b3e545b47bd7ca9b.jpg)

### Fig. 139: View Of Backing Plate & Nuts Rear Courtesy ofGENERAL MOTORS CORP.
`chevrolet_trailblazer_en_a7dba6a6` | trailblazer | pdf_manual | tier:4 | lang:en | layer:general | p:1 | doc_type:service_manual

8. Remove the retaining nuts from the backing plate.
9. Remove the backing plate from the rear axle housing.

### Fig. 140: View Of Backing Plate & Nuts Rear Courtesy ofGENERAL MOTORS CORP.
`chevrolet_trailblazer_en_ced2a071` | trailblazer | pdf_manual | tier:4 | lang:en | layer:brakes | p:1 | doc_type:service_manual

1. Install the backing plate to the axle housing flange.

NOTE: Refer to Fastener Notice in Cautions and Notices.

2. Install the backing plate nuts.

Tighten: Tighten the backing plate nuts to 135 N.m (100 1b ft).

![](images/8522aa81e7b0b8847706e91e83b641526f4ebc4a6465579e1741c3bc581760f1.jpg)
Fig. 141: View Of Rear Axle Assembly Courtesy of GENERAL MOTORS CORP.

3. Install the rear drive axle. Refer to Rear Axle Shaft Replacement in Rear Drive Axle.

![](images/34a45e6edc0e56762cce6c49003cf49a503b6f2e6ebb7c7cc688135fc5f5f742.jpg)
Fig. 142: View Of Park Brake Shoe Courtesy of GENERAL MOTORS CORP.

4. Install the park brake shoe.

![](images/ca2e8567db40ccf335999fc19f2284798aca62d7fc4f336a2b9b154bfcc3cb4b.jpg)
Fig. 143: View Of Rotor To Hub Courtesy of GENERAL MOTORS CORP.

5. Install the rear brake rotor.

![](images/dce050caca908519fd752d7935bae17b70369699bf6ce0b516be8c35d3519dde.jpg)
Fig. 144: View Of Caliper Bracket Courtesy of GENERAL MOTORS CORP.

6. Install the brake caliper and bracket assembly Refer to Brake Caliper Bracket Replacement Rear.

![](images/01d925f3c9643b214d1a53b8849fa60e408376e287fe197b36fee5467cd3add5.jpg)
Fig. 145: View Of Parking Brake Cable Courtesy ofGENERAL MOTORS CORP.

7. Install the park brake cable to the rear brake caliper. Refer to Park Brake Cable Replacement in Park Brake.

8. Adjust the park brake shoe. Refer to Park Brake Adjustment in Park Brake.

9. Install the tire and wheel assembly Refer to Tire and Wheel Removal and Installation in Tires and Wheels.

10. Lower the vehicle. Refer to Lifting and Jacking the Vehicle in General Information.

### Tools Required
`chevrolet_trailblazer_en_31a004e7` | trailblazer | pdf_manual | tier:4 | lang:en | layer:general | p:1 | doc_type:service_manual

S 41013 Rotor Resurfacing Kit. See Special Tools and Equipment.
J 42450-A Wheel Hub Resurfacing Kit. See Special Tools and Equipment.

IMPORTANT: Do NOT refinish the brake rotors in order to correct any of the following complaints:

Growl or squeal brake noise
Premature brake lining wear
Cosmetic orsuperficial corrosion of the rotor braking
surface
Rotor discoloration

Refinish the brake rotors ONLY when 1 or more of the following conditions exist:

Groove depth in excess of specification
Severe scoring of the rotor braking surface
Brake pulsation caused by the following: 。 Lateral runout in excess of specification 0 Brake rotor thickness variation in excess of specification 。 Corrosion or pitting that is deeper than the rotor braking surface
1. Use the J 42450-A in order to clean the wheel bearing/hub. See Special Tools and Equipment.
2. Use a micrometer in order to measure the thinnest point of the brake rotor. If the thinnest point of the brake rotor exceeds the brake rotor minimum thickness, do NOT refinish the brake rotor. Replace the brake rotor.
3. Use the J 41013 in order to THOROUGHLY clean the rust from the brake rotor flange. See Special Tools and Equipment.
4. Refinish the brake rotor. Refer to the brake lathe manufacturer's operating instructions.

IMPORTANT: Failure to obtain the best possible braking surface finish may cause the vehicle to stop with difficulty.

5. After machining the rotor, use l20 grit aluminum oxide sandpaper and a nondirectional rotor finisher, if available, in order to create a non-directional braking surface.
6. Clean the braking surfaces with GM P/N 12377981 (Canadian P/N 10953463) or equivalent, brake parts cleaner.

Callouts For Fig. 9

### DIAGNOSTIC INFORMATION AND PROCEDURES
`chevrolet_trailblazer_en_b32d94ba` | trailblazer | pdf_manual | tier:4 | lang:en | layer:general | p:1 | doc_type:service_manual

DIAGNOSTIC STARTING POINT DOORS

Begin the system diagnosis with the Diagnostic System Check Door Systems. The Diagnostic System Check will provide the following information:

The identification of the control module(s) which command the system.
+ The ability of the control module(s) to communicate through the serial data circuit.
The identification of any stored diagnostic trouble codes (DTCs) and their status.

The use of the Diagnostic System Check will identify the correct procedure for diagnosing the system and where the procedure is located.

### Test Description
`chevrolet_trailblazer_en_02a54c00` | trailblazer | pdf_manual | tier:4 | lang:en | layer:general | p:1 | doc_type:service_manual

The number(s) below refer to the step number(s) on the diagnostic table.

2: Lack of communication may be due to a partial malfunction of the class 2 serial data circuit or due to a total malfunction of the class 2 serial data circuit. The specified procedure will determine the particular condition.
5: The presence of DTCs which begin with "U" indicate some other module is not communicating. The specified procedure will compile all the available information before tests are performed.

### SCAN TOOL DATA DEFINITIONS
`chevrolet_trailblazer_en_7b980d85` | trailblazer | pdf_manual | tier:4 | lang:en | layer:general | p:1 | doc_type:service_manual

The Passenger Door Module (PDM) scan tool data definitions are a brief description of each of the related parameters displayed within the scan tool data display(s) for the PDM.

### Back Only Heat Indicator
`chevrolet_trailblazer_en_f28a9801` | trailblazer | pdf_manual | tier:4 | lang:en | layer:general | p:1 | doc_type:service_manual

This data parameter appears in two separate data-lists Inputs and Outputs. While monitoring the inputs for the PDM this parameter reflects the requested state for the back only indicator. When monitoring the outputs the parameter reflects the commanded state for the back only indicator.

### Back Only Heat Mode
`chevrolet_trailblazer_en_9f217523` | trailblazer | pdf_manual | tier:4 | lang:en | layer:general | p:1 | doc_type:service_manual

This data parameter reflects the operating state of the heated seat back only mode of operation. This mode of operation is only available if the heated seat is active or on.

### Backlighting Dimming Enabled
`chevrolet_trailblazer_en_721b4d5d` | trailblazer | pdf_manual | tier:4 | lang:en | layer:general | p:1 | doc_type:service_manual

This data parameter indicates the status feature. Backlighting is the lighting from behind any text or characters on the PDM switches or faceplate.

### Base Software P/N
`chevrolet_trailblazer_en_552ef677` | trailblazer | pdf_manual | tier:4 | lang:en | layer:general | p:1 | doc_type:service_manual

This is the part number stored within the PDM of the base software set required to activate the PDM This software set is often referred to as boot software.

### Battery 1 (Low Current)
`chevrolet_trailblazer_en_b2c5471e` | trailblazer | pdf_manual | tier:4 | lang:en | layer:general | p:1 | doc_type:service_manual

The battery 1 (low current) parameter provides the voltage level on the battery 1 input. 1 Battery is the low current input provides the power for the PDM's microprocessors and the low current outputs.

### Battery 2 (High Current)
`chevrolet_trailblazer_en_227b5614` | trailblazer | pdf_manual | tier:4 | lang:en | layer:general | p:1 | doc_type:service_manual

![](images/f8023c5ce1d07d1f3bb41d8eb02d401a40d9bdab1a797d1655d868ae5a0c45bb.jpg)

The battery 2 (high current) parameter provides the voltage level on the battery 2 input. Battery 2 is the high current input provides the power for the PDM's high current outputs such as the power windows.

### Calibration P/N
`chevrolet_trailblazer_en_dd682324` | trailblazer | pdf_manual | tier:4 | lang:en | layer:general | p:1 | doc_type:service_manual

This is the part number stored within the PDM of the calibration set. The calibration software sets the operational ranges and option levels for each of the PDM's functions. This software set is often referred to as calibration software or calibration.

### Calibration Suffix
`chevrolet_trailblazer_en_f4463555` | trailblazer | pdf_manual | tier:4 | lang:en | layer:general | p:1 | doc_type:service_manual

This suffix number is the stored indication of the revision level of the calibration This is often referred to as the CAL ID.

### Dimming Potentiometer
`chevrolet_trailblazer_en_2a78ba1f` | trailblazer | pdf_manual | tier:4 | lang:en | layer:general | p:1 | doc_type:service_manual

Thc dimming potcntiomctcr data paramctcr rcflccts thc lcvcl of lighting,in perccnt for both the backlighting and indicators for the PDM.

### Heat Indicator High
`chevrolet_trailblazer_en_bc404cc5` | trailblazer | pdf_manual | tier:4 | lang:en | layer:general | p:1 | doc_type:service_manual

This data parameter appears in two separate data-lists inputs and outputs. While monitoring the inputs for the PDM this parameter reflects the requested state for the heat indicator low. When monitoring the outputs the parameter reflects the commanded state for the heat indicator low.

### Heat Indicator Low
`chevrolet_trailblazer_en_bc404cc5` | trailblazer | pdf_manual | tier:4 | lang:en | layer:general | p:1 | doc_type:service_manual

This data parameter appears in two separate data-lists inputs and outputs. While monitoring the inputs for the PDM this parameter reflects the requested state for the heat indicator low. When monitoring the outputs the parameter reflects the commanded state for the heat indicator low.

### Heat Indicator Medium
`chevrolet_trailblazer_en_20f57d30` | trailblazer | pdf_manual | tier:4 | lang:en | layer:general | p:1 | doc_type:service_manual

This data parameter appears in two separate data-lists inputs and outputs. While monitoring the inputs for the PDM this parameter reflects the requested state for the heat indicator medium. When monitoring the outputs the parameter reflects the commanded state for the heat indicator medium.

### Mirror 5 Volt Reference
`chevrolet_trailblazer_en_79bf44f0` | trailblazer | pdf_manual | tier:4 | lang:en | layer:general | p:1 | doc_type:service_manual

This data parameter provides a status indication of the 5-volt reference circuit for the OSRV position sensors.

### Mirror Fold Motor Current
`chevrolet_trailblazer_en_00f8bd08` | trailblazer | pdf_manual | tier:4 | lang:en | layer:general | p:1 | doc_type:service_manual

This parameter provides the detected amount of current (in mA) that is required to perform the active mirror fold action.

### Mirror Motor
`chevrolet_trailblazer_en_bf8e486b` | trailblazer | pdf_manual | tier:4 | lang:en | layer:general | p:1 | doc_type:service_manual

This parameter provides information regarding the state of operation the mirror is currently activating.

### Mirror Switch Backlight
`chevrolet_trailblazer_en_8c8b5150` | trailblazer | pdf_manual | tier:4 | lang:en | layer:general | p:1 | doc_type:service_manual

The mirror switch backlight data parameter reflects the level of backlighting, in percent for the mirror switch.

### Window Motor Current
`chevrolet_trailblazer_en_266cb4f1` | trailblazer | pdf_manual | tier:4 | lang:en | layer:body | p:1 | doc_type:service_manual

![](images/813ad19d289127ae9c5aa63936179d5be8d87e883379b5526bf6ebd8db0da3fe.jpg)

This parameter indicates the active status of the window lockout feature.

### Window Switch Backlight
`chevrolet_trailblazer_en_9325560e` | trailblazer | pdf_manual | tier:4 | lang:en | layer:general | p:1 | doc_type:service_manual

The window switch backlight data parameter reflects the level of backlighting, in percent for the window switch.

Diagnostic Trouble Code (DTC) List
![](images/3acd719538606e9b749de226060296e778519f72d811409c2573a8ad103954a9.jpg)

### DTC B1017 OR B3970 (PASSENGER DOOR MODULE (PDM))
`chevrolet_trailblazer_en_367ff797` | trailblazer | pdf_manual | tier:4 | lang:en | layer:body | p:1 | doc_type:service_manual

Circuit Description

The Passenger Door Module (PDM), upon receipt of a Class 2 message or a direct lock or unlock switch input will supply battery positive voltage to the internal door lock and unlock relays control circuits.

### Conditions for Running thc DTC
`chevrolet_trailblazer_en_d00390aa` | trailblazer | pdf_manual | tier:4 | lang:en | layer:body | p:1 | doc_type:service_manual

![](images/07536d30e876a6711a1a0533baf4fef97c0218786bbe01852a327be8ad83dd13.jpg)

![](images/183daeb91236650f410e3421b1d44deb6bce7897a7dbee290483bd47cd35c64e.jpg)

Battery is voltage between 10.6 and 16.0 volts. Ignition switch in the ON position

Conditions for Setting thc DTC

When the PDM supplies battery voltage to the internal door lock relay control circuit and it is shorted to ground for more then 160 milliseconds.
When the PDM supplies battery Voltage to the internal door unlock relay control circuit and it is shorted to ground for more then 160 milliseconds.

Action Taken When the DTC Sets

The PDM will cancel any outputs on the door lock or unlock relay control circuits for the remainder of the ignition cycle.

### Conditions for Clearing the DTC
`chevrolet_trailblazer_en_e2092d96` | trailblazer | pdf_manual | tier:4 | lang:en | layer:general | p:1 | doc_type:service_manual

When the fault is no longer present. the PDM changes the current DTC to a history DTC.
The PDM will clear the history DTC after 50 fault free ignition cycles.

DTC B1017 or B3970 (Passenger Door Module (PDM))
![](images/79aaa979471143a518d96f17b76cba97a3cfdc6d716dbdbf9d9a1ec9a8bd9613.jpg)

![](images/4351944ab517a98d30da90f4560fcc8d74984d2a3a5798777eb9193de301588d.jpg)

![](images/747c54dd1ea6d95ee4a219e34423b25e7b9fb16d15353f66a2dab7f2b6b5808a.jpg)

![](images/dc324c1fa014276c651037e55f660330829b264c874981ed29e88f5467417461.jpg)

### Circuit Description
`chevrolet_trailblazer_en_e11957f6` | trailblazer | pdf_manual | tier:4 | lang:en | layer:body | p:1 | doc_type:service_manual

The Driver Door Module (DDM), upon receipt of a Class 2 message will supply battery positive voltage to the internal mirror heater relay control circuit to enable the mirror heater relay. The DDM will also supply a pulse width modulated signal on the heated/memory seat switch illumination circuit to control switch back lighting.

### Conditions for Running thc DTC
`chevrolet_trailblazer_en_b493a652` | trailblazer | pdf_manual | tier:4 | lang:en | layer:general | p:1 | doc_type:service_manual

Battery voltage is between 10.6 and 16.0 volts. Ignition switch in the ON position

Con ditions for Sctting thc DTC

When the DDM supplies battery voltage to the internal mirror heater relay control circuit and it is shorted to ground for more then 160 milliseconds. When the DDM supplies a pulse width modulated signal on the heated/memory seat switch illumination circuit and it is shorted to ground for more then 160 milliseconds.

Action Taken When the DTC Sets

The DDM will cancel any outputs on the internal mirror heater relay control circuit and the heated/memory seat switch illumination circuits for the remainder of the ignition cycle.

### Conditions for Clearing the DTC
`chevrolet_trailblazer_en_a4d90143` | trailblazer | pdf_manual | tier:4 | lang:en | layer:general | p:1 | doc_type:service_manual

When the fault is no longer present, the DDM changes the current DTC to a history DTC.

![](images/05d1938cdb2687fc82dfe97a697a9c6413265c27fdb6ae84c111efd011eaf6cd.jpg)

The DDM will clear the history DTC after 50 fault free ignition cycles.

### Test Description
`chevrolet_trailblazer_en_532b3c05` | trailblazer | pdf_manual | tier:4 | lang:en | layer:general | p:1 | doc_type:service_manual

The numbers below refer to the step numbers on the diagnostic table.

2: This step determines if the heated/memory seat switch illumination circuit is the cause for DTCB1017 setting.
3: This step determines if the mirror heating element supply voltage circuit is the cause for DTC B1017 setting.
4: This step eliminates the possibility that the heated/memory seat switch is internally shorted to ground

### Circuit Description
`chevrolet_trailblazer_en_4bfaf0b9` | trailblazer | pdf_manual | tier:4 | lang:en | layer:general | p:1 | doc_type:service_manual

The switch that failed and generated this diagnostic trouble code (DTC) is part of the DDM or the PDM. When a switch is activated, the DDM/PDM then either actuates the appropriate component or sends a message over the serial data link to the appropriate module to actuate the appropriate component.

![](images/83490539aad50a4b826e7a594d97cad8c2c93ed812d76982169d68a927d540fc.jpg)

![](images/a0f31f128d0d390e91c32e7a9595a8803054e248e9d79144113b3be07c85c391.jpg)

### Conditions for Running the DTC
`chevrolet_trailblazer_en_1575c8ab` | trailblazer | pdf_manual | tier:4 | lang:en | layer:body | p:1 | doc_type:service_manual

System voltage is between 9.0 and 16 volts. Ignition switch is in the ON position

Conditions for Setting the DTC

If the DDM or the PDM receives multiple inputs from a single switch at the same time for more then 160 milliseconds, the DDM/PDM sets the DTC. The following switches will set this DTC:

The door lock switch The power window switch The window lockout switch

Action Taken When the DTC Sets

The DDM/PDM ignores the continuous inputs from the switch The DDM/PDM will not respond to the invalid inputs as a command for the component. The DDM/PDM will respond to all other valid switch inputs.

### Con ditions for Clearing thc DTC
`chevrolet_trailblazer_en_ad25e714` | trailblazer | pdf_manual | tier:4 | lang:en | layer:general | p:1 | doc_type:service_manual

When the switch input status changes, the DDM/PDM clears this current DTC and stores the DTC as a history DTC. The history DTC will clear either after 50 ignition switch cycles without a repeated failure or in response to a scan tool command.

### Circuit Description
`chevrolet_trailblazer_en_7c979f6a` | trailblazer | pdf_manual | tier:4 | lang:en | layer:body | p:1 | doc_type:service_manual

The driver door module supplies battery positive voltage to the power mirror switch. When the mirror select switch is placed in the left or right mirror position, the appropriate signal circuit is switched to 12 volts. The driver door module senses the voltage change and allows control of the selected outside rearview mirror.

### Conditions for Running theDTC
`chevrolet_trailblazer_en_898874cf` | trailblazer | pdf_manual | tier:4 | lang:en | layer:body | p:1 | doc_type:service_manual

Battery voltage must be between 9.0-16.0 volts.

![](images/524d94abcfa581a5ac27028d5fb0665181b1af258c7eb7768681de17da6f913c.jpg)

The ignition switch is in the ON position.

Conditions for Setting the DTC

The driver door module senses an active state on both mirror select switch circuits greater than 3 seconds.

### Action Taken Whcn thc DTC Scts
`chevrolet_trailblazer_en_bab12da8` | trailblazer | pdf_manual | tier:4 | lang:en | layer:general | p:1 | doc_type:service_manual

The driver door module ignores the continuous inputs from the switch. The driver door module will respond to all other valid switch inputs.

### Conditions for Clearing thc DTC
`chevrolet_trailblazer_en_52888c5b` | trailblazer | pdf_manual | tier:4 | lang:en | layer:general | p:1 | doc_type:service_manual

When the fault is no longer present, the door module changes the current DTC to a history DTC. The door module will clear the history DTC after 50 fault free ignition cycles, or in a response to a scan tool command.

`chevrolet_trailblazer_en_d61f78b4` | trailblazer | pdf_manual | tier:4 | lang:en | layer:body | p:1 | doc_type:service_manual

The door modules supply battery positive voltage and ground on opposing mirror motor control circuits based on the position of the mirror switch.

### Conditions for Running thc DTC
`chevrolet_trailblazer_en_1eefbae7` | trailblazer | pdf_manual | tier:4 | lang:en | layer:general | p:1 | doc_type:service_manual

System voltage must be between 9.0-16.0 volts.
The ignition switch must be in the ON position.

Conditions for Sctting thc DTC

If the mirror motor right control circuit is shorted to battery Voltage and the mirror is
commanded left.
1 If the mirror motor down control circuit is shorted to battery voltage and the mirror is
commanded up.
+ If the outside rearview mirror motor down control circuit is shorted to ground and the mirror is commanded down. If the outside rearview mirror motor right control circuit is shorted to ground and the mirror is commanded right.

### Action Taken When the DTC Sets
`chevrolet_trailblazer_en_0b22d415` | trailblazer | pdf_manual | tier:4 | lang:en | layer:general | p:1 | doc_type:service_manual

![](images/3f822a38cbbe6dc5a556fc6dcd8ade85085b2f6e4c5343edf34ff10f8aed9735.jpg)

The outside rearview mirror will not operate properly on all axes.

When the fault is no longer present, the door module changes the current DTC to a history DTC. The door module will clear the history DTC after 50 fault free ignition cycles, or in response to a scan tool command.

The door modules supply battery positive voltage and ground on opposing mirror motor control circuits based on the position of the mirror switch.

### Conditions for Running theDTC
`chevrolet_trailblazer_en_857dcce6` | trailblazer | pdf_manual | tier:4 | lang:en | layer:general | p:1 | doc_type:service_manual

![](images/d8482feac4d28bbbb35d4baadce30f6fb38bd6c16d6e81e3dbd11a4700b89eba.jpg)

The ignition switch must be in the ON position.

`chevrolet_trailblazer_en_fbc457fb` | trailblazer | pdf_manual | tier:4 | lang:en | layer:body | p:1 | doc_type:service_manual

If the mirror motor up/left control circuit is shorted to battery voltage and the mirror is commanded down or right.

When the fault is no longer present, the door module changes the current DTC to a history DTC. The door module willclear the history DTC after 50 fault free ignition cycles, or in response to a scan tool command.

### DTC B1724
`chevrolet_trailblazer_en_5029290b` | trailblazer | pdf_manual | tier:4 | lang:en | layer:general | p:1 | doc_type:service_manual

![](images/91c4ae459edcbe3434a16882aa78dbe3628680523431bc5793d520a2d360261c.jpg)

![](images/93e86b592a2e9d1013f8083104811df5979b035d3220f5f39ebe6c184e8dc414.jpg)

![](images/7cd3ccc96cfadbae6fabf3b4198c3befa07e790ac1cbd1a33432bec1aa4f51e7.jpg)

![](images/e333bc114baa0e79dee065307c908e2c6a09503d7b67f3dd121cc44dfd0e1e64.jpg)

### Circuit Description
`chevrolet_trailblazer_en_7acf3fb3` | trailblazer | pdf_manual | tier:4 | lang:en | layer:body | p:1 | doc_type:service_manual

The Body Control Module (BCM) intermally grounds the door lock relay control circuit for the rear door lock relay when it receives a Class 2 message from the Driver Door Module (DDM), Passenger Door Module (PDM) or the Liftgate Module (LGM) with a keyless entry transmission When the BCM grounds the door lock relay control circuit the rear door lock relay is energized and locks the rear doors.

### Conditions for Running thc DTC
`chevrolet_trailblazer_en_ba8e5486` | trailblazer | pdf_manual | tier:4 | lang:en | layer:body | p:1 | doc_type:service_manual

Battery voltage is between 9.0 and 16.0 volts.
The DTC will set if the rear door lock function is active or inactive.

### Conditions for Setting thc DTC
`chevrolet_trailblazer_en_fba4f27d` | trailblazer | pdf_manual | tier:4 | lang:en | layer:body | p:1 | doc_type:service_manual

The DTC will set if the door lock relay control circuit is shorted to ground or open for more then one second.

### Action Taken When the DTC Sets
`chevrolet_trailblazer_en_cc231018` | trailblazer | pdf_manual | tier:4 | lang:en | layer:body | p:1 | doc_type:service_manual

+ If a short to ground is present on the door lock relay control circuit, the door lock relay will be energized and the rear door lock actuators will be held in the lock position and will not unlock.
+ If an open condition is present on the door lock relay control circuit, the rear doors will unlock but not lock.

### Conditions for Clearing the DTC
`chevrolet_trailblazer_en_ffa4308e` | trailblazer | pdf_manual | tier:4 | lang:en | layer:general | p:1 | doc_type:service_manual

When the fault is no longer present, the BCM changes the current DTC to a history DTC.
The BCM will clear the history DTC after 50 fault free ignition cycles.

### Diagnostic Aids
`chevrolet_trailblazer_en_1798919a` | trailblazer | pdf_manual | tier:4 | lang:en | layer:electrical | p:1 | doc_type:service_manual

DTC B3808 could also set if the door lock relay winding is open.
If both DTC B3808 and B3809 are set as current, the battery positive voltage circuit or
the LOCKS fuse could be open If the LOCKS fuse is open, the cause is most likely
one of the following: + A short to ground in the relay battery positive voltage circuit. A short to ground in the door lock actuator lock or unlock control circuits.

### Test Description
`chevrolet_trailblazer_en_c21b4097` | trailblazer | pdf_manual | tier:4 | lang:en | layer:body | p:1 | doc_type:service_manual

The number(s) below refer to the step number(s) on the diagnostic table.

2: Listen for an audible click when the rear door lock relay operates. Command both the ON and OFF states Repeat the commands as necessary.
3: Tests for voltage at the coil side of the rear door lock relay. The LOCKS fuse supplies power to the coil side of the rear door lock relay.
4: Verifies that the body control module is providing ground to the rear door lock relay. 5: Tests if ground is constantly being applied to the rear door lock relay.

![](images/104e4b38465c55e1efc784efb7e953eca03805c14160805b0e4f4339e2f3bac0.jpg)

![](images/8a8ec9b034b42c41a613b4a428dab13c8d82a7a70e64bf77f5c69378e4342ef6.jpg)

### Circuit Description
`chevrolet_trailblazer_en_e49af117` | trailblazer | pdf_manual | tier:4 | lang:en | layer:body | p:1 | doc_type:service_manual

The Body Control Module (BCM) internally grounds the door unlock relay control circuit for the rear door unlock relay when it receives a Class 2 message from the Driver Door Module (DDM), Passenger Door Module (PDM) or the Liftgate Module (LGM) with a keyless entry transmission. When the BCM grounds the door unlock relay control circuit the rear door unlock relay is energized and unlocks the rear doors.

### Conditions for Running thc DTC
`chevrolet_trailblazer_en_b9c6a0d9` | trailblazer | pdf_manual | tier:4 | lang:en | layer:body | p:1 | doc_type:service_manual

Battery voltage is between 9.0 and 16.0 volts.
The DTC will set if the rear door unlock function is active or inactive.

Conditions for Sctting thc DTC

The DTC will set if the door unlock relay control circuit is shorted to ground or open for more then one second.

### Action Taken When the DTC Sets
`chevrolet_trailblazer_en_f9bed364` | trailblazer | pdf_manual | tier:4 | lang:en | layer:body | p:1 | doc_type:service_manual

If a short to ground is present on the door unlock relay control circuit, the door unlock relay will be energized and the rear door lock actuators will be held in the unlock position and will not lock.
If an open condition is present on the door unlock relay control circuit, the rear doors will lock but not unlock.

### Diagnostic Aids
`chevrolet_trailblazer_en_9a4ef953` | trailblazer | pdf_manual | tier:4 | lang:en | layer:electrical | p:1 | doc_type:service_manual

DTC B3809 could also set if the door unlock relay winding is open.
If both DTC B3808 and B3809 are set as current, the battery positive voltage circuit or
the LOCKS fuse could be open. If the LOCKS fuse is open, the cause is most likely
one of the following: ↓ A short to ground in the relay battery positive voltage circuit. A short to ground in the door lock actuator lock or unlock control circuits.

### Conditions for Clearing the DTC
`chevrolet_trailblazer_en_1aecc55a` | trailblazer | pdf_manual | tier:4 | lang:en | layer:general | p:1 | doc_type:service_manual

![](images/f50b1a88c1d82336ea58da467b97abaa1361b6942a39b5f34d4c131b4a95af3c.jpg)

When the fault is no longer present, the BCM changes the current DTC to a history DTC.
The BCM will clear the history DTC after 50 fault free ignition cycles.

### Test Description
`chevrolet_trailblazer_en_82301890` | trailblazer | pdf_manual | tier:4 | lang:en | layer:body | p:1 | doc_type:service_manual

The number(s) below refer to the step number(s) on the diagnostic table.

2: Listen for an audible click when the rear door unlock relay operates. Command both the ON and OFF states. Repeat the commands as necessary.
3: Tests for voltage at the coil side of the rear door unlock relay. The LOCKS fuse supplies power to the coil side of the rear door unlock relay.
4: Verifies that the body control module is providing ground to the rear door unlock relay.
5: Tests if ground is constantly being applied to the rear door unlock relay.

### Circuit Description
`chevrolet_trailblazer_en_8318f4d8` | trailblazer | pdf_manual | tier:4 | lang:en | layer:body | p:1 | doc_type:service_manual

The Body Control Module(BCM) grounds the control side of the left rear power window up relay upon receiving a class 2 message from the Driver Door Module (DDM).

### Conditions for Running the DTC
`chevrolet_trailblazer_en_36a5ca1d` | trailblazer | pdf_manual | tier:4 | lang:en | layer:general | p:1 | doc_type:service_manual

Battery voltage is between 9.0 and 16 volts
The BCMhas received a class 2 message from the DDM and has grounded the left rear power window relay control circuit.

Con ditions for Setting thc DTC

When the BCM has grounded the left rear power window up relay control circuit and the control circuit is shorted to battery positive voltage.
The above condition is met for 250 milliseconds.

Action Taken When the DTC Sets

The left rear power window will be inoperative from both the driver and left rear power window switches.

Command both the ON and OFF states. Repeat the commands as necessary.

4: Verifies that the BCM is providing ground to the left rear power window up relay.

### DTC B3820
`chevrolet_trailblazer_en_b3329cf4` | trailblazer | pdf_manual | tier:4 | lang:en | layer:body | p:1 | doc_type:service_manual

Circuit Description

The Body Control Module (BCM) grounds the control side of the left rear power window down relay upon receiving a class 2 message from the Driver Door Module (DDM).

Conditions for Running the DTC

Battery voltage is between 9.0 and 16 volts.
The BCM has received a class 2 message from the DDM and has grounded the left rear power window down relay control circuit.

Conditions for Setting the DTC

When the BCM has grounded the left rear power window down relay control circuit and the control circuit is shorted to battery positive voltage.
The above condition is met for 250 milliseconds.

Action Taken When the DTC Sets

The left rear power window will be inoperative from both the driver and left rear power window switches.

### Conditions for Clearing the DTC
`chevrolet_trailblazer_en_ffa4308e` | trailblazer | pdf_manual | tier:4 | lang:en | layer:general | p:1 | doc_type:service_manual

When the fault is no longer present, the BCM changes the current DTC to a history DTC.
The BCM will clear the history DTC after 50 fault free ignition cycles.

### Test Description
`chevrolet_trailblazer_en_9f638755` | trailblazer | pdf_manual | tier:4 | lang:en | layer:general | p:1 | doc_type:service_manual

The numbers below refer to the step numbers on the diagnostic table.

2: Listen for an audible click when the left rear power window relay operates.
Command both the ON and OFF states. Repeat the commands as necessary.
3: Tests for voltage at the coil side of the left rear power window down relay.

4: Verifies that the BCM is providing ground to the left rear power window down relay.

5: Tests if ground is constantly being applied to the left rear power window down relay.

DTC B3820

### Circuit Description
`chevrolet_trailblazer_en_5c924188` | trailblazer | pdf_manual | tier:4 | lang:en | layer:body | p:1 | doc_type:service_manual

Battery positive voltage is supplied to the left rear power window switch and the up and down relays through the window lockout signal circuit. If the window lockout function is disabled, the BCM supplies battery positive voltage to the switch and relays and the left rear window operates normally. If the window lockout function is enabled, the BCM will not supply battery positive voltage to the switch and relays and the left rear window will be inoperative unless it is operated from the driver power window switch.

### Conditions for Running theDTC
`chevrolet_trailblazer_en_21ed4f62` | trailblazer | pdf_manual | tier:4 | lang:en | layer:body | p:1 | doc_type:service_manual

Battery voltage is between 9.0 and 16.0 volts. The window lockout function is disabled

Conditions for Sctting thc DTC

The window lockout function is disabled and the window lockout signal circuit is shorted to ground.
The above condition must be met for 250 milliseconds.

Action Taken When the DTC Sets

The left rear power window will be inoperative from both the driver and passenger power window switches.

### Conditions for Clearing the DTC
`chevrolet_trailblazer_en_86d36b2e` | trailblazer | pdf_manual | tier:4 | lang:en | layer:general | p:1 | doc_type:service_manual

When the fault is no longer present, the BCM changes the current DTC to a history DTC.
The BCM will clear the history DTC after 50 fault free ignition cycles.

### DTC B3822
`chevrolet_trailblazer_en_dbda1b16` | trailblazer | pdf_manual | tier:4 | lang:en | layer:body | p:1 | doc_type:service_manual

Circuit Description

The Body Control Module (BCM) grounds the control side of the right rear power window up relay upon receiving a class 2 message from the Driver Door Module (DDM).

Conditions for Running the DTC

Battery voltage is between 9.0 and 16 volts.
The BCMhasreceived a class 2 message from the DDM and has grounded the right rear power window relay control circuit.

Conditions for Setting the DTC

When the BCM has grounded the right rear power window up relay control circuit and the control circuit is shorted to battery positive voltage.
The above condition is met for 250 milliseconds.

Action Taken When the DTC Sets

The right rear power window will be inoperative from both the driver and right rear power window switches.

### Conditions for Clearing the DTC
`chevrolet_trailblazer_en_ffa4308e` | trailblazer | pdf_manual | tier:4 | lang:en | layer:general | p:1 | doc_type:service_manual

When the fault is no longer present, the BCM changes the current DTC to a history DTC.
The BCM will clear the history DTC after 50 fault free ignition cycles.

5: Tests if ground is constantly being applied to the right rear power window up relay.

### IMPORTANT: The following steps must be completed before using the symptom tables.
`chevrolet_trailblazer_en_13889d1c` | trailblazer | pdf_manual | tier:4 | lang:en | layer:body | p:1 | doc_type:service_manual

1. Perform the Diagnostic System Check Door Systems before using the Symptom Tables in order to verify that all of the following are true:

There are no DTCs set.
The control module(s) can communicate via the serial data link.

2. Review the system operation in order to familiarize yourself with the system functions. Refer to the following system descriptions:

Power Windows Description and Operation
Power Door Locks Description and Operation
Outside Mirror Description and Operation (Power Mirrors) or Outside
Mirror Description and Operation (Heated Mirrors)

### Visual/Physical Inspection
`chevrolet_trailblazer_en_46b5cda7` | trailblazer | pdf_manual | tier:4 | lang:en | layer:body | p:1 | doc_type:service_manual

Inspect for aftermarket devices which could affect the operation of the power door lock, power window and power mirror systems]. Refer to Checking Aftermarket Accessories in Wiring Systems.
Inspect the easily accessible or visible system components for obvious damage or conditions which could cause the symptom.
On power window systems, inspect for obstructions or binding conditions which may cause the circuit breaker to open and reset.

### Intermittent
`chevrolet_trailblazer_en_3db2968b` | trailblazer | pdf_manual | tier:4 | lang:en | layer:electrical | p:1 | doc_type:service_manual

Faulty electrical connections or wiring may be the cause of intermittent conditions. Refer to Testing for Intermittent Conditions and Poor Connections in Wiring Systems.

On power window systems, obstructions or binding conditions may cause an intermittent condition where the circuit breaker opens and resets.

### Diagnostic Aids
`chevrolet_trailblazer_en_61f7708e` | trailblazer | pdf_manual | tier:4 | lang:en | layer:body | p:1 | doc_type:service_manual

Power window motors have a built-in circuit breaker which protects the motor. The circuit breaker may open when there isa window obstruction, binding condition or the power window switch has been activated for an extended period of time. The circuit breaker will reset automatically as the circuit breaker cools, however 5-15 seconds may elapse before the circuit breaker resets.
